Seven billion dollars is exported to Russia, while Russia's exports to Turkey are $25 billion. About 3,500,000 Russian tourists arrived in Turkey last year. Valuable MPs, the 7th Amendment (b) of the Law contains only situations that make the employment of a certain gender mandatory. With gender discrimination, this clause provides an area that can be abused to the employer and an unlimited initiative. In addition, women and men have equal rights, especially in Article 10 of the Constitution. The state is obliged to ensure that this equality is granted to life. In much harder jobs, in the field, in the garden, women have been working since ancient history. We have women working hard as forest workers. There will surely be conditions for which they will be forced, and professions where their physical strength will not be sufficient. It's not just for women, it's for men. The protection and development of human rights are not only provided by signing contracts on rights and freedoms, but also by the existence of mechanisms that allow contracts to function, and the need for these mechanisms to function fairly and effectively. In this context, the protection and development of human rights on the axis of relations between the individual and the state is as important as any state has a duty to do so with what methods and mechanisms. Dear lawmakers have uncovered the obligation to implement human rights violations, international human rights standards on a national level, to be followed, and to co-operate with international protection mechanisms. The national human rights institutions play an important bridge between the rulers and the rulers, while providing good potential to bring international standards to a national level. Dear lawmakers, these institutions, whose duties have been to protect and improve rights, receive rights violation applications and complaints, study and research, have become severely criticised bodies, which are strongly emphasised by the European Union's decision and document on the need for membership. In this context, the Paris Principles are known as recommendations and guide principles developed to encourage governments to create a most suitable and efficient human rights institution. As a matter of fact, the Paris Principles attribute special importance to the representation of different parts of society within these institutions in order to ensure the independence and pluralism of human rights institutions. One of the main functions expected from human rights institutions is to gather representatives of the government under a roof to find solutions to human rights issues with different parts of society. Dear lawmakers, in order to ensure the independence of human rights institutions, it is imperative that the members of the institution be employed in pluralistic ways. It is also a minimum requirement for civil society organisations, unions, social and professional organisations, academics, jurisprudences and other experts to be represented in a pluralistic way in the selection of board members working in the field of human rights. The election of human rights institutions by members of the board is essential to a democratic procedure, but where we come from, the current minimum symbolic pluralist-looking applications are also being eliminated, and eight of the 11 members are being appointed by the Council of Ministers and the President, as well as the power of the board to elect presidents are being handed over to the government directly. Dear lawmakers, this is a clear indication that the institution, which is supposedly founded in harmony with the Paris Principles, does not have the right to independence. Almost all of the members of the board and the presidents of the board must be appointed by the government, and Turkey is meant to mock public opinion and universal law norms. However, increasing the number of authorities to vote in the way that each office chooses fewer people will allow more communities to take part in the process, which will give the institution considerable transparency and dignity. tbmm-2016-03-31sit07spe0590par0427	Mr. President, Congressmans, I have made a pledge on behalf of my group on Bill 149. I salute the High Council with respect to human rights. As you know, the United Nations Universal Declaration of Human Rights was adopted in 1949 after the great human destruction of World War II. In signing this declaration, it was effective that after World War II, states merged to secure rights and freedoms for individuals. Unfortunately, it was understood after millions of people lost their lives that the countries realized that they could no longer get anywhere by fighting. Given the duration of that time, we are now reliving a very similar process all over the world. The reason for the inextricable situation in the Middle East is that as a country, there is also a problem with not focusing on the essence of the human concept. Instead of being a little more hostile with extreme rhetoric, we need to show the will to live together in the human denominator. We need to stop seeing our differences as a threat to each other. Dear friends, we must learn to stop competing with our pain and to share our pain. We must end the search to make those who can't see all the pain that they can't see. At this point, as a physician, I'd like to emphasize that this feeling of pain is a human trait in solving problems. As you know, the first signs that there's a problem with metabolism are understood by a sense of pain. Patients who feel this pain first seek help from us physicians to find a solution. Now, there's also the social dimension of this pain. The way this pain feels its own existence, unfortunately, causes other problems that can lead to other sufferings. Right now, we all feel the pain of our martyrs in our hearts. I remember our martyrs with mercy, and to heal the wounded with haste, and to be patient with their relatives. The sense of pain in this social dimension is the trigger for a violent spiral with physiological reflections. To put an end to this, we need to learn to think only about people. Congressmans, here we are, the physicians of this social pain, we are politicians. Come, let's not cause any more human destruction without making these pains more insurmountable and driving people away from the self-absorption. Human rights is a principle of life that you and I need today and tomorrow. For example, I have been wronged many times in my career, in my public office, in my political life, although I did not hurt even an ant as a guide to the words of your enemy. This has never caused me to deviate from my basic philosophy of life. But I can't deny that making human rights my philosophy of life has given me much. The work of experts on human rights and equality, which is a very important quality in this sense, is an important development, but we hope that these developments on paper will also be reflected in the application. It is our desire that experts who will work in this institution should be chosen from neutral people who feel human rights in their whole self and soul, who have the right to human rights philosophy. I salute the General Assembly.
